# Audit findings ledger

Carried across `/weekend-audit` runs by the `audit-loop` skill. **Never delete a row** — close it
with a date. A finding that reappears after being closed is a much stronger signal than a fresh
one, and only this file can show that. An open row surviving 3 runs is itself a finding.

This is the single shared backlog for every agent/model and all repository work, including
skill installation, research, deployment and user decisions. Plans and memory link these IDs.

Lanes: **FIX** (mechanically safe) · **EVIDENCE** (touches a score/weight/threshold — needs a
measurement first) · **INVESTIGATE** (symptom, unproven cause) · **ACCEPT** (known, bounded).

## Shared backlog registration index

Owned by AF-20261025-20 / AF-20261025-21. Source-comment registrations preserve an auditable
mapping; they are not completion claims. Historical implementation recipes now link here.
The individually registered unchecked bullets below are reusable runbook/verification templates,
not pending implementation tasks. A newly added bullet or changed source marker needs review.

Missing Greeks on NSE rows: AF-20261128-01. Open, investigated 2026-11-27 10:36-10:45 IST (read-only). (1) The NSE fallback AF-20260905-19 recorded as fixed was never wired: only its test imports `nse_option_chain_source`. (2) Wired as written it would corrupt the table: NSE open interest is in lots and stored values are shares (stored/NSE ratios 1,367-6,687, about the lot size; per-expiry lots are in `nt_fno_expiry`), and it puts the absolute OI change into `ce_oi_chg_pct` (the percent is `pchangeinOpenInterest`). (5) NSE answered 25 of 25 stale F&O names in 19 s. A correlation on six runs, not proven. **Owner ruling 2026-11-27: NSE fills gaps only** (no `source` column added; judged exception to the `data-sources.md` composite-key rule). Fixed the same day, pending the next live run (the 2026-11-27 evening `ml-daily-ops`): NSE is called for names with no chain that day and writes with `DO NOTHING`; OI is converted to shares with `nt_fno_expiry` lot sizes, the percent field is used, `&` is encoded in both sources, an earlier-session snapshot is not stored, and the universe is names with futures in the exchange file (220). NSE rows have NULL Greeks, so the stock page shows 0 for a Greek it has no other source for. Live dry run 10:46 IST (writes stubbed): 8 of 12 filled in 4.2 s; IEX, IREDA, ACUTAAS, APARINDS skipped for want of a lot size. Basis checked 10:51 IST on M&M and CANBK, both sources seconds apart: chain OI totals within 0.05%, NSE strikes a subset (SmartOptions-only strikes hold 0 OI), max pain and ATM identical. `so_stock_oi_summary` differs a little by source: `pcr` 1-3% (SmartOptions publishes its own figure) and ATM call IV 0.4-0.7 points, which `feature_engineering` reads for IV skew. NiftyTrader checked 10:56-11:05 IST as an alternative chain (user asked): `option/option-chain-data` answers without headers but returns a strike window (25-48 strikes, `atmBelow/atmAbove=100` did not widen it; call OI 2-4% short of the full chain on M&M and CANBK), volume in shares, and delta and IV 0 on every row with and without login (only `calls_iv_eod` filled), so NSE stays the gap source. IEX, IREDA, ACUTAAS, APARINDS are not in its `psymbol-list`, so it gives no lot size for them. Other sources checked 11:01-11:03 IST after commit `c865346a`: the registry has no Greeks route and the `urls-explorer` option-chain rows for Tickertape, StockEdge, ET, Sensibull and `mcapi/v1/option-chain` all record HTTP 404; Moneycontrol `get-option-details` returned 114 CANBK strikes with premium and IV all null. NSE `fo_mktlots.csv` has lot sizes for ACUTAAS (150) and APARINDS (50), which `nt_fno_expiry` lacks; IEX and IREDA are not in it and their last contract expired 11-23 (the 14-day universe rule still lists them). Black-Scholes at 6.5% from SmartOptions IV reproduces its call Greeks on 628 strikes of 11-26 (median error delta 0.001, gamma 0.7%, vega 0.3%, theta 16%), so NSE-row Greeks could be computed with no new source (not built). Two follow-ups 11:18 IST (user asked): the universe requires an unexpired contract (218 live; IEX, IREDA out) and a lot size missing from `nt_fno_expiry` comes from NSE `fo_mktlots.csv`, which agreed with it on all 222 shared names; ACUTAAS and APARINDS filled in a stubbed live run (`test_universe_drops_names_whose_contracts_have_all_expired`, `test_a_lot_size_nt_does_not_carry_comes_from_the_exchange_file`, both failed first). Full-universe NSE pass 15:42 IST, writes stubbed (nothing stored): 218 of 218 names filled in 117 s (11-71 strikes each), no skips, the 10-failure stop did not trip, no overwrite; SmartOptions had written no 2026-11-27 rows yet. Truth = raw close x TEJ factors with an ex-date after the bar and not in the future; a bar is corrected only when truth over production equals a clean ratio (a product of TEJ factors, or a TEJ factor over the stored factor for that date) within 2%, in a contiguous run of at least 20 bars, with steps under 5% left alone; short holes between runs and unverifiable bars at a run's edges take the run's multiplier only while production stays continuous (never past the next ex-date); and a symbol is REFUSED for review if the correction would create a bar-to-bar step over 25% that was not there before. | AF-20261112-09 | 2026-11-12 | scheduling / feature-refresh-before-its-inputs | Owner: Claude; origin: same audit. dl-feature-refresh (17:00 IST) builds day D's feature_store row from technical_signals flow columns (pcr_oi, pcr_vol, iv_rank, iv_skew, block_deal_net_qty, call/put wall distance, near_expiry_gamma, sector_ret_5d/21d), which are written later: the grid at 18:50, pcr_fetcher/iv_features from 19:38. So the latest feature row is built before its inputs land and is only repaired by the next day's run. No effect on today's canonical ranking (ML is excluded from the blend by its calibration gate), so not moved blind. Also recorded, not changed: SWING/POSITIONAL valid_until carries the 22:30 generation clock rather than the last session's 15:30 close (deliberate, keeps measurement windows comparable); the technical grid (18:50) is computed on the 16:00 Yahoo bar before the 19:48 bhavcopy reconcile, while confluence levels read the reconciled close. | DECISION | **Update 2026-11-17 - measured before building; the defect is smaller than this row assumed, so no step was added.** The owner approved the latest-day rebuild. Checked first who reads the latest feature_store row: the live ensemble scorer (`ml_ensemble` score SQL) takes pcr_oi, pcr_vol, iv_rank, iv_skew, the walls, near_expiry_gamma, sector_ret and block_deal from `technical_signals` (`ts.*`), not from feature_store, so tonight's scoring already sees the evening values. Older feature_store rows are completed by the next 17:00 full refresh: on 2026-11-13 and 11-16 pcr_oi is 72.5% and 73.6% filled against 80.8% and 82.1% in technical_signals, while the newest row (2026-11-17) is 0.0%. The newest row is read only by drift_detector (one of its 30 recent dates) and by ranker_input_snapshot as a provenance record. Adding the rebuild inside ml-daily-ops costs about 25 min (the full refresh measured 1,700 s) on a chain that ran 201 min against its 234 min budget, which would push it to the edge that AF-20261112-10 warns about. Recommendation recorded for the owner: leave it, or run `feature_engineering.py --date today` as its own job after the ranker (about 22:45) if the drift reading matters. | AF-20260818-47 | 2026-08-18 | data-correctness | **`technical_signals.stop_loss`/`targets` store a ₹-formatted display string, not a number — and two different downstream readers expect a number.** Noticed incidentally in pm2 logs while verifying AF-46/AF-42: `outcome_resolver.py` erroring `invalid input syntax for type double precision: "₹60.33"` on every run since 2026-08-13, plus a live `/todays-picks` screenshot showing `SL ₹NaN`. Traced to `getTradingSetup()` (`technicalSignalsService.ts`, the 2026-08-13 deterministic replacement for the never-ran LLM insight generator): it formats `stopLoss`/`targets` as `"₹1239.39"` for the Telegram digest, and that same formatted string was written straight into `technical_signals.stop_loss`/`targets` — read back as numbers by `outcome_resolver.py`'s `CAST(... AS REAL)` and the frontend's `Number(pick.stop_loss)`. `targets` additionally expected a JSON array by the frontend; a bare `"₹65.00"` string always failed `JSON.parse`, so targets have never rendered since this feature shipped. | FIX | **fixed + live-verified + negative-controlled 2026-08-18.** `unifiedUpsertSql` already regex-extracted a clean number for this exact reason when writing `unified_signals.stop_loss` (`slNumeric`) — extracted that into a shared `parseMoneyToNumber()` helper and applied it to the `technical_signals` write too; `targets` now written as a JSON array of the extracted number. `entryZone` untouched (a genuine formatted range, nothing parses it numerically). **Repaired the 32 existing poisoned rows live** (all from that day's scan): `stop_loss` stripped to clean numeric text, `targets` converted to a JSON array — re-ran `outcome_resolver.py`'s exact `CAST` query after, 0 errors. Frontend hardened too: SL only renders when `Number.isFinite`, degrading silently instead of showing NaN on any future malformed value. Live-verified post-restart: `/todays-picks` shows real `SL`/`T1` values on all 20 picks (previously 0 ever rendered a target, and SL always read NaN) | **rung 4** — 4 new tests on `parseMoneyToNumber` (money string, thousands separator, null/undefined/empty, already-bare number) | 2026-08-18 |

## Run 2026-08-23 — e2e-lifecycle-check findings

Source: the `e2e-lifecycle-check` skill's first real runs. Notable because finding **AF-20260823-69
is a defect in the check itself**, and fixing it is what surfaced AF-20260823-70 — a recurring
production bug that had been invisible to every existing monitor for at least 7 weeks.


### Triage of AF-20260823-73's 6 non-ranker hits (2026-08-23)

Each site's source table was checked for weekend/holiday rows, and each empty-read traced to its
consumer. **The distinction that matters is not "does the read return empty" but "does the caller
DEGRADE or NO-OP when it does."** Two are real, three are benign, one is a true positive whose
source table saves it.

| ID | Site | Source table trading-day-only? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| AF-20260823-74 | `scoring_engine.py:688` (`days=1`) | **yes** — `technical_signals` 25 weekend rows / 32,894 in 21d | **REAL, and the worst of the six** |
| AF-20260823-75 | `scoring_engine.py:761` (`days=3`) | **yes** | **REAL on a long weekend only** |
| AF-20260823-76 | `screener_sector_rotation.py:52` (`days=2`) | **yes** | **REAL but self-limiting** |
| — | `screener_sector_rotation.py:23` (`YESTERDAY`, `days=1`) | n/a — reads its own output table | benign |
| — | `screener_signal_generator.py:84` (`days=2`) | **no** — `screener_appearances` writes ~20k rows every weekend day | benign |
| — | `ml_ensemble.py:3074` (`days=2`) | n/a — not a lookback at all | benign (checker false positive, by design) |

| ID | Found | Class | Finding | Lane | Status | Immunized | Closed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AF-20260823-74 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | **`scoring_engine.py:688`'s `win_prob_map` cutoff is `date.today() - 1` over `technical_signals`.** On any Monday the map comes back **empty**, and `win_prob_map.get(symbol)` then returns `None` for every symbol — which `ml_alignment_points()` converts to its `8  # neutral if no ML signal` fallback. **Measured live: the real mean is 17.71/20** (2,196 symbols on the latest date), so Factor 3 silently drops **~9.7 of 20 points on every symbol, uniformly, every Monday.** A uniform shift cannot change any *ranking*, which is exactly why it is invisible — but `stock_scores` feeds `unified_recommendations` and the platform's absolute thresholds, the same mechanism as `recurring-bugs.md`'s factor-crowding incident (a uniform ×0.9 that moved the whole population against fixed cutoffs). Same file also uses the correct pattern elsewhere, so this is an inconsistency, not a house style. | **EVIDENCE** | **CLOSED same day — see "AF-20260823-70/74/75/76 CLOSED 2026-08-23" below.** 0 → 2,196 symbols, before/after on a live production connection. Row reconciled 2026-08-29 (previously contradicted the closure block below for 6 days). | rung 1 — `check_short_calendar_lookback` + rung 4 `test_trading_day_cutoffs.py` | 2026-08-23 |
| AF-20260823-75 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | `scoring_engine.py:761`'s `sym_signal_types` cutoff is `days=3` over `technical_signals` — survives a normal Fri→Mon gap (exactly 3) but returns empty after any **long weekend / holiday Monday** (4+ calendar days). Consumer is the `win_prob_map and sym_signal_types` prior-blend at :784, which is skipped entirely when either is empty — so the blend silently does not run rather than running wrong. | EVIDENCE | **CLOSED same day — see closure block below.** 566 → 722 symbols before/after. Row reconciled 2026-08-29. | rung 1 + rung 4 | 2026-08-23 |
| AF-20260823-76 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | `screener_sector_rotation.py:52`'s primary read is `days=2` over `technical_signals` → empty on Monday. **Self-limiting**: the function's own docstring documents a `screener_appearances` fallback, and that table *does* write on weekends, so the script degrades to its secondary path rather than producing nothing. Output table `screener_sector_rotation` is fresh (max 2026-08-22, 533 rows), consistent with the fallback working. | ACCEPT | **CLOSED same day — see closure block below.** Fixed anyway (cheap, not gated): primary path now fires on Mondays instead of always falling back to the secondary path. Row reconciled 2026-08-29. | rung 1 + rung 4 | 2026-08-23 |

**Three benign, with reasons — recorded so they are not re-triaged next run:**

- **`ml_ensemble.py:3074`** — `cutoff_date` is **not a lookback filter**. It is passed to
  `_apply_plain_expiry_gate`/`_apply_regime_expiry_gate` and used as `if str(r['signal_date']) <
  cutoff_date: expire`, i.e. an age threshold for expiring `recommendation_log` rows whose
  `win_probability` is NULL. An empty trading day just means marginally fewer rows expire that
  run, self-correcting the next day. **Checker true-positive on shape, false-positive on impact** —
  the check flags `date.today() - timedelta(days<=4)` regardless of use, which is the right
  trade (shape is cheap and reliable to detect; intent is not).
- **`screener_sector_rotation.py:23`** — `YESTERDAY` reads the script's **own output table**
  (`load_previous_sector_scores`) for a momentum delta, not a trading-day source. Missing
  yesterday's row yields `{}` → momentum_change of 0, which is correct behaviour for "no prior
  snapshot".
- **`screener_signal_generator.py:84`** — source is `screener_appearances`, which is **not**
  trading-day-only: ~19-29k rows land on every weekend day (13,080 weekend rows in the last 21d).
  The 2-day window always contains data.

The EVIDENCE gate was satisfiable without waiting for a Monday: **2026-08-23 was a Sunday, so the
bug was live at the moment of the fix.** Both engines were exercised against production before and
after, on the same connection, minutes apart — not simulated, not reasoned about.

`win_prob_map` at 0 is Factor 3 sitting on its `8  # neutral` fallback for every symbol against a

stages), which it could not do before the fix — `engine:dl` was FAILing. That is the end-to-end
proof, not just a green suite.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AF-20260823-74 | 2026-08-23 | temporal | `scoring_engine.py:688` `win_prob_map` days=1 → Factor 3 collapses to 8/20 every Monday | EVIDENCE→FIX | **fixed + live-verified**, 0 → 2,196 symbols | rung 1 + rung 4 | 2026-08-23 |

## 2026-08-23 — options-wall zero-vs-NULL (AF-77 fixed, AF-78 open)


**Sweep query that found AF-78** (re-run it after any new `DEFAULT 0` column lands):

```sql
SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_name='technical_signals' AND table_schema=current_schema()
  AND column_default IN ('0','0.0','(0)::double precision','(0)::real')
  AND data_type IN ('real','double precision','numeric','integer','bigint','smallint');
```

Then per column: `count(*) FILTER (WHERE col=0)` vs `count(DISTINCT col)`. A high zero-share with
`count(DISTINCT) > 2` on a **continuous** quantity is the signature; a 2-valued column is a flag
and almost always fine.

**AF-78 fix landed 2026-08-23**: schema defaults dropped (`db/schema.postgres.sql`) and both
grid/backfill INSERTs in `backfill_technical_features.py` now supply explicit NULLs for
`fii_10d_net` / `dii_3d_net` / `sector_ret_5d` / `sector_ret_21d` instead of omitting them.
Residual risk moved upstream: the FII/DII **fetcher** still populates very few symbols
(live 08-23: distinct≈2/day) — that is now a data-coverage problem, not a schema problem.

| AF-20260827-14 | 2026-08-27 | performance | `npm run smoke:ci`: `getModelRocDiagnostics` timed out after 15000ms. Real production impact, not a code bug: live `unified_recommendations`/`technical_signals` reads were stalled for real users during this window. | INVESTIGATE | **Closed 2026-11-28.** The unguarded sites were about fifty modules, not ten, so the guard is on the db_compat engine: `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` on an existing column is skipped (no lock) and a real add waits at most 5s for the lock. Every module with such an ALTER uses that engine. Live probe on `technical_signals`: 0 AccessExclusive locks held. Long-running Python services pick it up at their next restart. AF-20260917-24 root cause found for AF-20 (missing unified_recommendations 09-17): the post-close job cluster exhausts the Postgres connection pool, and ml-daily-ops burns its ENTIRE budget under that contention. Evidence, all live read-only 2026-09-18: (a) logs/pm2-out.log holds 3,863 `timeout exceeded when trying to connect` events since 09-11, of which 545 fall in the single hour 23:37 IST on 09-17 (vs 22 at 21:xx, 32 at 20:xx) -- a ~25x spike exactly at the window's worst moment; (b) job_run_history shows four unrelated jobs sharing the identical completion second 2026-09-17 23:37:56 (screener-performance, screener-performance-compute, live-screener-ml-train, and screener-performance's 22:10 sibling) -- a process-kill signature, not coincidence; three more share 2026-09-18 01:34:20; (c) ml-daily-ops ran 21:35:09->01:05:09 IST for exactly 210.0 min = its full 3.5h withJobTimeout, then failed with '13 steps failed' -- marketsmojo_technical_fetcher, iv_features, reconcile-stock-ohlcv, relative_strength, ownership_relative, insider_features, intraday_features, avwap_features, oi_delta_features, fno_rollover_fetcher, delivery_volume_fetcher, block_deal_fetcher, mc_price_features_ohlcv (the report text also logs 'exceeded its 210min execution budget - failing the job to free the worker slot'); (d) consequence confirmed at the source: fno_rollover max(date)=2026-09-16 while every other FNO/institutional table is 09-17 (stock_delivery_data 09-17, block_deals 09-17), so the ranker's FNO-rollover input was one day stale; reconcile-stock-ohlcv independently timed out at its own 300000ms ceiling; (e) unified_recommendations has ZERO rows for 2026-09-17 (max generated_at 2026-09-16 17:31:15Z, computed_at already rolled to 09-17 by logical_session_date, so the table is labelled for a session it has no ranking for). Overlap map (>=3min, IST): ml-daily-ops 21:35-01:05, screener-performance 22:10-23:37 & 22:51-23:37, job-digest 22:50-23:33, data-quality-daily 23:00-23:40, quant-eod-sync 22:00-22:35, news-sentiment 5x overlapping, trendlyne-catchup 8x (~39 min cumulative, from its `*/20 * * * *` all-day cadence in trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts:267), unified-ranker 23:00-23:30. The schedule is serialized only by queue concurrency, not by resource: these are distinct Queues with their own workers running in one Node process against one Postgres, so their Python children contend for the same connection ceiling. Note ml-daily-ops' own registration comment (queues.ts:2439) asserts it 'cannot run past 16:50 UTC' because of its 3.5h timeout from 13:20 -- true only if it STARTS on time; it started 45 min early on a catchup run and the scheduled run then waited on concurrency:1, so the documented worst-case bound does not hold and the 18:00 UTC ml-weekly-data slot's stated safety margin is unverified. Fixes applied this session (tsc exit 0, 360/360 vitest pass in jobRegistryCronMirror + jobPipelineOrdering + jobRegistryGraceMinutesConsistency + dataQualityChecks): unified-ranker budget 30->45 min with lock 35->55 min and a bounded 15-min make-up run on failure (AF-20); recommendations-digest now guards against consuming a stale ranker table; dataQualityChecks' ohlcv-bar-plausibility no longer conflates quarantined is_suspect rows with genuinely malformed bars; market_holidays seeded. NOT YET DEPLOYED -- the 09-17 failure executed the pre-fix 30-min budget (its error text is literally 'Timed out after 1800000ms'), so these edits are unloaded and the make-up path has never run. Remaining, needs user decision: ml-daily-ops' budget/steps must be split or parallelised (13 failing steps in a 210-min serial chain cannot fit), the 23:00-01:30 IST window needs resource-level staggering (trendlyne-catchup's all-day 20-min cadence and data-quality-daily's unindexed scans on 17M-row tables are both avoidable load in exactly that window), and market_holidays still has future=0 rows (max 2026-09-14) so H2-2026 weekday backfill decisions remain unguarded. Evidence: scratch_verify/audit_probe6_out.txt, audit_probe7_out.txt, scratch_verify/audit_probe10_out.txt, scratch_verify/cols_out.txt.

Both jobRegistry cronPatterns updated in lockstep (jobRegistry.ts trendlyne-catchup + data-quality-daily entries) so jobRegistryCronMirror stays green; registerRepeatableJob's wipe-and-readd (registerJob.ts getRepeatableJobs/removeRepeatableByKey) makes the change clean on boot with no stale-key double-firing.

**AF-20260919-01 (ml-daily-ops 2-step failures) — ROOT-CAUSED & FIXED 2026-09-19**

Found: 2026-09-19 during verification of AF-24 fix. After pool-exhaustion restructure, ml-daily-ops reduced from 13-step cascade to exactly 2 persistent failures: mc_chart_patterns_fetcher and iv_features (stock IV pass). Live 2026-09-18: 21:56 IST run failed with these two. Historic: 2026-09-15 mc_chart_patterns failed solo, 2026-09-16 ml-ensemble-score failed solo, pre-09-15 was succeeding, so these are not perennial.

**Root cause, both confirmed the same class as AF-24 (residual, not a new mechanism):**
1. **`mc_chart_patterns_fetcher.py`** — measured live: runs in **3.9s** clean (bulk fetch 0.26s + upsert 1.47s) against its **120s** budget, i.e. **30x headroom**. Budget is provably not the constraint. The failure is a transient `db_compat.connect()` timeout at script start under momentary Postgres `max_connections` saturation from a concurrently-running sibling job — the exact mechanism AF-24 diagnosed (3,863 "timeout exceeded when trying to connect" events in pm2 logs since 09-11), just an unlucky single script rather than a 13-way cascade.
2. **`iv_features.py` (stock IV pass)** — measured live: runs in **28.5s** clean against its **90s** budget (only **3.2x** headroom, thin by this repo's own established floor for once-daily batch steps). This call (queues.ts line ~1072) is a SEPARATE, second invocation of `iv_features.py` from the one AF-24's fix batch raised (line ~759, already at a 10min floor) — the fix batch missed this second call site.

**Fixes applied 2026-09-19:**
1. `db_compat.connect()` (src/server/db_compat.py) now retries `OperationalError`/`SQLAlchemy TimeoutError` up to 3 attempts with short backoff before raising — fixes the class at its single shared source (167 call sites benefit) rather than patching one caller, matching this repo's own precedent (`db_compat.reconnect()`'s docstring: "a guard re-typed per call site is a guard that will be missing from the next call site"). A persistent outage still raises after 3 attempts — not silently swallowed.
2. `iv_features.py` (stock IV pass) budget raised 90s → 5min (queues.ts line ~1072), matching the floor AF-24 already established for comparably-light steps.

**Evidence:** live timing (mc_chart_patterns 3.9s, iv_features 28.5s); 4 new negative-controlled tests in `src/server/tests/test_db_compat_connect_retry.py` (transient OperationalError retried-then-succeeds, transient SATimeoutError retried-then-succeeds, persistent failure raises after exactly 3 attempts — not swallowed, unrelated exception type propagates immediately without retry); existing `test_db_compat_reconnect.py` (6/6) and `test_db_compat_now_utc.py` still pass; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `jobRegistryCronMirror`+`jobPipelineOrdering`+`jobRegistryGraceMinutesConsistency` 228/228 pass (budget change did not desync any cron-mirror/grace-window guard).

Lane: FIX → CLOSED. Not blocking the ranker at any point (both are enrichment steps). Watch next 3 ml-daily-ops runs for recurrence of either failure; if `mc_chart_patterns_fetcher` still fails after the connect() retry, that would indicate the transient window is longer than 3 short-backoff attempts can absorb and needs a longer backoff, not a different mechanism.

**data-quality-daily's other 2026-09 failures, triaged in the same pass (no code change needed):**
- `fundamentals-history-vendor-field-decay` — **intentionally red**, per its own in-code comment ("It is meant to be red right now"). Tracks Yahoo's still-decayed ROE field (6.1% coverage); the ensemble-side fix is correctly EVIDENCE-lane-blocked on AF-20260913-07's Saturday `ml-weekly-retrain` measurement window. Not a bug — do not suppress this check.
- `ohlcv-fabricated-session` (fired 09-12, 09-16) and `unified-recommendations-ghost-symbols` (fired 09-14) — both **currently read 0 live** (re-ran each check's exact SQL 2026-09-19: 0 bad days in last 90d, 0 ghost symbols). These are the write-side guards from AF-20260911-15/AF-20260914-05 catching real, occasional, self-resolving anomalies — working as designed, not a persistent defect.
- `ohlcv-bar-plausibility` — last failed 2026-09-17 (before the 09-18 08:39 IST deploy that fixed it per AF-24's session note); zero recurrences since. Fix verified holding.

## AF-20260913-07 addendum — the ROE-swap arm specifically (still open, 2026-09-20)

**Do not conflate with the null-guard sub-question closed immediately above — they are different arms of the same row.** Re-checked 2026-09-20 in response to `fundamentals-history-vendor-field-decay` still reading critical/fail in the daily digest:

- **The DL-path fix already landed** (2026-09-18, `feature_engineering.py:420-443`): `feature_store.roe` fills Yahoo's NaN gaps from `investsights_fundamentals_history.return_on_equity` (Pearson 0.9608, zero sign flips), COALESCE-direction so pre-decay history is untouched. `test_roe_investsights_fallback.py` exists and covers it. This consumer is done.
- **The ensemble-path swap has NOT run.** `ml_ensemble.py` still reads `fh.return_on_equity` directly off `fundamentals_history` at every query site (lines 243, 1244, 1504, 1775, 2076, 2358, 2692 — grepped 2026-09-20, unchanged) — deliberately, per this row's own plan, pending a coordinated retrain-and-compare. **The 2026-09-19 `ml-weekly-retrain` (model_registry id 342, `20260919_115848`, cv_roc_auc 0.5313) trained WITHOUT this arm** — it is an ordinary weekly retrain on the still-decayed raw column, not the measured comparison this row calls for. So the "next `ml-weekly-retrain` window" this row has cited since 2026-09-13 has now passed twice (09-19, and 09-12/09-14 before it) without the ROE arm actually running.
- **Why this session did not run it either:** wiring `investsights_fundamentals_history.return_on_equity` into `ml_ensemble.py`'s live training/scoring query is a scoring-input change requiring the retrain-and-compare-on-`factor_edge_history` measurement this row already specifies, not a mechanical FIX — attempting it ad hoc mid-session, outside the coordinated `ml-weekly-retrain` window, risks exactly the train/serve population-shift risk this row was written to avoid (a model splitting on ROE=0 suddenly scored against dense values, or vice versa if only one side is retrained).
- **`fundamentals-history-vendor-field-decay` staying critical/red every night is correctly working as designed**, not a bug: `queues.ts`'s `data-quality-daily` deliberately throws on any critical DQ failure specifically so an unresolved critical issue keeps showing up in the job digest rather than being buried in a once-daily Telegram line (see that file's own comment). Suppressing the check or downgrading its severity would hide a real, still-open, permanent vendor decay (Yahoo will not restore this field) whose one remaining consumer fix is legitimately gated on a measurement, not neglect.

**Unblock condition, restated precisely:** the NEXT `ml-weekly-retrain` invocation that is deliberately run with (a) `ml_ensemble.py` pointed at the InvestSights-backed ROE fallback for both training and scoring, and (b) the pre-fill-coverage null guard from this row's original finding, compared against the incumbent (id 342) on realized `factor_edge_history` — not merely "the next Saturday retrain happens to fire." Approximate date: the following scheduled `ml-weekly-retrain` window (Sat 2026-09-26 05:00 UTC) IF the code change lands before then; otherwise the row stays open past that date too. This is a `needs a user decision` / EVIDENCE row, not calendar-blocked by elapsed time alone — nothing stops the code change from landing sooner than the next Saturday.

(2) **NOT A BUG, closed:** the "5th outcomes table" claim misapplies `scoring-authority.md`'s cap — that rule's own text names exactly four SIGNAL-outcome tables (`unified_signals`/`technical_signals`/`signal_outcomes`/`unified_signal_outcomes`) and explicitly rejects merging even a 5th *candidate into that family* on its merits; `market_decision_outcome` is a structurally different thing (a bitemporal decision-audit-trail table in a new subsystem, not a signal/outcome-grading table), so the cap doesn't apply to it by the rule's own scope. Full event-sourcing (a row per change) is deliberately NOT done — that is a schema change and is not required to stop the loss. **A second, independent bug found while fixing it:** `sync_identity` passed `nse_stocks.last_updated` as `available_at`, which is not just mutable but frequently **EARLIER** than the sync that wrote the row — the dangerous direction, since it claims we knew a fact before we did and a point-in-time read at that instant returns a row that did not yet exist (look-ahead). Now `None`, letting the writers' own `COALESCE(..., now())` stamp the sync's real time. Docstring corrected from "append-only" to "immutable-provenance upsert" with an explicit do-not-re-add note. **Item 3(b) (zero-weight engines labeled supports/contradicts) remains open** — lowest-value of the set, deliberately deferred, unchanged. **Source changes landed in `6fd9ba72`** (swept into a concurrent session's ontology commit, so the reasoning lives here rather than in that commit message). | AF-20260927-18 | 2026-09-27 | ml-model | **`technical_signals.win_probability` has NO single horizon target, so "is it calibrated" is ill-posed — and this is the root cause under both AF-20260927-08 and the regime gate's whole design.** Proven live, not inferred: of 71,142 `(symbol, signal_date)` pairs on the outcome join, **71,142 carry exactly ONE distinct `win_probability`**, and **23,456 of them are graded against more than one horizon**. The realized WIN base rate is a property of the horizon — **38.2% @1d / 64.1% @5d / 78.8% @15d** — so one number is being asked to predict three different events. Measured consequence: `mean_p` is nearly horizon-flat (0.50–0.66) while truth swings 0.31→0.88, which makes the same column simultaneously **over-confident at 1d and under-confident at 15d**. The Brier-optimal shrinkage per (regime, horizon) shows it directly — HIGH_VOL reads **−0.244 / +0.925 / +0.973** at 1d/5d/15d, i.e. the required correction flips sign across horizons of the same regime. No single per-regime weight can be right for all three, so AF-20260927-08's fix (a measured per-regime slope) is a genuine improvement AND a ceiling: it fits one number to a three-target quantity. **This also explains the news-sentiment result** (below) landing only at 15d. **The real fix is upstream:** give `win_probability` one defined horizon, or emit one probability per horizon (the gate has no horizon dimension available at scoring time — `edge_adjusted_probability(p, regime, ...)` never sees a horizon — so it cannot be fixed at the gate). Tested rather than assumed. **Leak check first (`ml-model-bugs.md` standing rule): `corr(news_sentiment_score, same-day return) = +0.0072` on n=32,426 — passes the <0.2 bar**, so the score is not contaminated by the move it would be predicting. One point in its favour worth keeping: BOTH buckets contain news, so both should be volatile, which makes the differential look directional rather than the volatility artifact that contaminates the HIGH_VOL AUC. **Why not built:** at h=15 with daily overlapping windows, 4,839 rows over ~60 dates is **~4 effective observations** — LOW-DATA by this file's own bar; the bad-news buckets are 125–241; only ~21.8% of the panel carries a non-zero news score (45,288 of 66,850 non-null values are exactly 0); and conditioning the weight on a PER-STOCK variable would break the cross-sectional uniformity, converting the gate from a sizing adjustment into an alpha claim that must clear a cost-aware `factor_backtest.py` bar it has never faced. Do NOT wire news into `regime_edge_weight` before a cost-aware run. | Leak check n=32,426 `corr=+0.0072`; win-rate and `w*` splits from the 21,562-row non-zero-news panel (script in session scratch). | |

### 2026-10-01 — signal closure, validity windows and win-rate readers

Merge state matters here: `-32/-33/-35/-36/-38` are on main (e006a741, 814a825e, 00ede6b8, eb9ac311, 8c9d9d8b) and `-37` is on main **unwired** (372ea455). `-30/-31/-34/-39` are committed on `worktree-agent-ae3e77c1b361d6a0a` and deliberately **not merged**: they touch `outcome_resolver.py`, `jobs/operations.jobs.ts` and `intraday_outcome_resolver.py`, which a concurrent session is editing (37 files, 1,555 lines uncommitted at 2026-10-01).


### Decisions taken 2026-10-01 (user: "do what is logically correct as per stock markets")

| # | Question | Decision and market reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Expiry: stop fabricating (agent branch) **or** sweep every horizon (concurrent session's `expire_stale_pending_all`)? | **Stop fabricating.** A trade whose price history exists must be labelled from real prices — a time exit books the last close, not a constant 0.0%. All 67,647 fabricated rows were priceable, and 95% of each day's batch become gradeable once AF-39 lands. Sweeping more horizons calls the same fabricating function, so it would spread the defect to h2/3/7/14/30 and re-apply it after every relabel. The concurrent session's real observation (h7 never resolved) is a **resolution** gap owned by `confluence_outcome_tracker`, fixed under AF-33 — not an expiry gap. |
| D2 | Grade the TA engine's universe-wide trend labels (~2,419/day) as trades? | **No — they are a factor, not a trade.** An EMA20/EMA50 state published for every listed stock is a cross-sectional feature; attaching a stop and a target and calling stop-outs "losses" produced 21,374 STOP_LOSS vs 3,241 WIN at h5 and says nothing about trade accuracy. Keep writing forward-return labels for research/ML (that is the correct supervised target for a factor), and keep `signal_source='technical'` of `unified_signals` out of every trade-accuracy surface. The landed win-rate readers already scope to `signal_outcomes` (the selective scanner) + path_barrier, so no user-facing number includes the panel today; this records the invariant. |
| D3 | Publish short trade plans for Sell/Strong Sell rows (326 with no geometry)? | **No short geometry in cash equity.** Indian cash settlement does not permit a multi-day short; only intraday MIS or F&O. A Sell on this platform means **exit / avoid**, not "go short", so a mirrored stop/target would be un-actionable for the audience that reads it, and grading it as a short trade would invent a position nobody can hold. Leave Sell rows without trade geometry and treat them as exit signals. Revisit only for F&O-eligible names with a stated product decision. |
| D4 | Reset the optimizer's noise weights and the reward weights pinned at floor/cap? | **Reset the noise weights (AF-43), leave the EMA weights alone (AF-46).** `sector`/`other`/the three source weights have no data column in the optimizer's objective, so their saved values (other 1.8244, sector 1.2249, ETnow 1.0737) are unconstrained search output, not learned information — defaults are strictly more honest. The 745/896 weights pinned at 0.3/2.0 are a **formulation** error (the EMA targets the raw reward instead of a 1.0-centred multiplier); resetting without reformulating just re-pins them, and the reformulation is EVIDENCE-lane. |
| D5 | Historical `calibrated_win_probability` values fitted in-sample? | **NULL them for dates before the fix.** `backtester.py` reads the column historically, so an in-sample probability inflates every backtest that touches it; consumers already fall back to the raw probability, which is the honest input. |
| D6 | The 31k graded outcomes of withdrawn (INVALIDATED_CONFLICT) screener signals? | **Delete, with a CSV backup.** The platform itself withdrew those calls, so they are not trade outcomes; leaving them in drags every screener win rate (3.3% vs 6.5% on the live set). Blocked twice by the auto-mode classifier — needs the user to run it. |
| D7 | `screener_signal_generator.py`, 0 signals since 2026-08-13? | **Unschedule it** (AF-18 stays open pending that edit). Its gate admits 32/33 screeners that are *Unranked* — they pass on a 0.386 prior, i.e. no track record — while every screener that has one falls below the bar. A generator that can only fire on unproven screeners is not a signal source; raising/lowering the threshold is EVIDENCE-lane and must be measured, not guessed. |
